## Manajemen Ekosistem & Networking Kewirausahaan (MENOKEN)

### Universitas Cenderawasih

You are a senior full-stack software engineer, product architect, database architect, UI/UX designer, and data analytics engineer.

Your task is to design and incrementally develop a production-oriented web application named:

# MENOKEN

**MENOKEN = Manajemen Ekosistem & Networking Kewirausahaan**

MENOKEN is a digital entrepreneurship ecosystem platform for Universitas Cenderawasih (UNCEN).

This application must NOT be designed merely as an administrative data-entry system.

MENOKEN must function as an integrated:

1. Entrepreneurship Database
2. Entrepreneurship Program Management System
3. Entrepreneurship Tracking System
4. Entrepreneurship Monitoring & Evaluation System
5. Entrepreneurship Performance Dashboard
6. Business Development Platform
7. Student Product Marketplace / Digital Catalog
8. Entrepreneurship Impact Measurement System
9. Entrepreneurship Networking Ecosystem
10. Decision Support System for university leadership

The long-term goal is to transform entrepreneurship management at Universitas Cenderawasih from:

**Scattered Data → Integrated Data**

**Administration → Tracking**

**Activities → Measurable Outcomes**

**Manual Reports → Real-Time Dashboard**

**Hidden Student Products → Digital Marketplace**

**Annual Programs → Sustainable Entrepreneurship Ecosystem**

---

# 1. CORE PRODUCT PHILOSOPHY

MENOKEN must follow the principle:

## DATA-DRIVEN ENTREPRENEURSHIP

Every activity must generate data.

Every relevant data point must generate measurable indicators.

Every indicator must provide information.

Every information output must support evaluation and decision-making.

The application should represent the entrepreneurship development chain as:

**INPUT → PROGRAM → PROCESS → OUTPUT → OUTCOME → IMPACT → DECISION**

DATA must serve as the foundation connecting all stages.

MENOKEN must answer not only:

> "How many students participated?"

but also:

> "What happened to their businesses after participating?"

The system should be able to measure:

* business survival;
* business development;
* legal compliance;
* product development;
* revenue growth;
* market expansion;
* digitalization;
* partnerships;
* employment creation;
* entrepreneurship program outcomes;
* social/economic impact.

---

# 2. CORE DATA PRINCIPLE

Implement:

# ONE GROUP – ONE DIGITAL PROFILE

Every student business group must have one primary digital profile serving as its central source of truth.

A business group must be traceable through:

**Profile → Members → Program Participation → Proposal → Selection → Training → Funding → Mentoring → Legalities → Products → Sales/Revenue → Demo Day → Festival → Partnerships → Impact**

Do NOT create isolated modules with duplicated group data.

Use relational references so all entrepreneurship records can be traced back to the same business group.

The digital profile must display the group's complete entrepreneurship history.

---

# 3. USER ROLES & RBAC

Implement secure Role-Based Access Control.

Minimum roles:

### 1. SUPER ADMIN

Can:

* configure the system;
* manage users;
* manage roles and permissions;
* manage master data;
* manage faculties;
* manage study programs;
* manage entrepreneurship categories;
* manage system configurations;
* manage scoring configuration;
* view audit logs;
* access all modules.

### 2. ADMIN / ENTREPRENEURSHIP MANAGER

Can:

* manage entrepreneurship programs;
* manage student business groups;
* verify data;
* manage proposals;
* manage reviewers;
* manage training;
* manage funding;
* manage mentoring;
* verify legalities;
* verify products;
* manage events;
* manage documentation;
* monitor business development;
* generate reports;
* view operational dashboards.

### 3. OPERATOR

Can perform operational data management according to permissions granted by Admin/Super Admin.

### 4. STUDENT / BUSINESS GROUP

Students can:

* create/manage business profile;
* manage group members;
* submit proposals;
* upload documents;
* view selection status;
* update legalities;
* add products;
* update prices;
* submit revenue data;
* submit business development data;
* upload documentation;
* submit social media accounts;
* add external marketplace links;
* view mentoring;
* view program status;
* update business data periodically.

One student should be identified as the group leader.

### 5. REVIEWER

Can:

* access assigned proposals;
* score proposals;
* provide comments;
* submit evaluation;
* view assigned review history.

Reviewer must NOT automatically have access to unrelated sensitive student information.

### 6. MENTOR

Can:

* access assigned businesses;
* record mentoring sessions;
* record problems;
* record interventions;
* create recommendations;
* create follow-up actions;
* update follow-up status.

### 7. UNIVERSITY LEADERSHIP

Read-only or analytical access.

Can access:

* executive dashboard;
* aggregate indicators;
* trends;
* comparisons;
* reports;
* outcome and impact indicators.

Leadership does NOT need access to unnecessary student personal data.

### 8. PUBLIC USER

Can access only public information such as:

* MENOKEN Market;
* approved business profiles;
* approved products;
* public entrepreneurship stories;
* public events.

Never expose private student data.

---

# 4. MASTER DATA

Create configurable master-data management.

Minimum master data:

* Faculties
* Study Programs
* Academic Years
* Students
* Business Categories
* Business Stages
* Business Statuses
* Legal Document Types
* Entrepreneurship Programs
* Funding Sources
* Training Categories
* Document Categories
* Product Categories
* Partnership Categories
* Event Types
* Scoring Indicators

Do not hard-code values that may change.

Admin must be able to manage configurable master data.

* Business status

Default examples:

1. Idea
2. Pre-startup
3. Startup
4. Operating
5. Growing
6. Independent
7. Scale-up

Store:

* Study program
* Entry year

* Skills

* Active/inactive status

* faculty;
* study program;
* academic year;
* business category;
* entrepreneurship program.

Avoid duplicating student records if the same student participates in multiple programs.

# 7. ENTREPRENEURSHIP PROGRAM MANAGEMENT

Admin must be able to create entrepreneurship programs for different years.

Example:

**Program Kewirausahaan Mahasiswa Universitas Cenderawasih 2026**

Program workflow may contain:

**Registration → Proposal Submission → Administrative Verification → Review → Selection → Announcement → Training → Funding → Mentoring → Monitoring → Demo Day → Entrepreneurship Festival → Evaluation → Post-Program Tracking**

Program stages should be configurable whenever practical.

Each business group's participation must have a status and history.

---

# 8. PROPOSAL MANAGEMENT

Students must be able to submit proposals online.

Proposal data should include:

* business name;
* group;
* leader;
* members;
* faculty;
* study program;
* background;
* problem;
* solution;
* products/services;
* target market;
* Business Model Canvas;
* activity plan;
* budget plan;
* revenue target;
* production target;
* proposal document;
* supporting documents.

Implement submission status:

**Draft → Submitted → Administrative Review → Eligible / Ineligible → Under Review → Accepted / Rejected**

Maintain timestamps and history.

---

# 9. REVIEW & SELECTION SYSTEM

Implement multi-reviewer scoring.

Configurable scoring indicators may include:

* Innovation
* Market Potential
* Business Feasibility
* Sustainability
* Social Impact
* Economic Potential
* Proposal Quality
* Team Readiness

Admin must be able to configure:

* indicators;
* weights;
* scoring range;
* reviewer assignments.

Each reviewer evaluates independently.

The system calculates weighted scores and final scores automatically.

Provide:

**Ranking → Score → Status → Reviewer Notes**

Do not hard-code scoring formulas into UI components.

Store scoring configuration in the database.

---

Manage entrepreneurship training.

Examples:

* Business Model Canvas
* Digital Marketing
* Branding
* Packaging
* Financial Literacy
* Business Legalities
* Taxation
* Business Management
* Marketplace
* Pitching
* Public Speaking
* Sustainability

Store:

* training title;
* category;
* date;
* location;
* speaker/trainer;
* participants;
* attendance;
* materials;
* certificates;
* documentation.

Calculate:

**Attendance Rate = Attended Participants / Registered Participants × 100**

# 11. FUNDING MANAGEMENT

Record entrepreneurship funding.

Support multiple funding transactions where necessary.

Dashboard must show:

* total funding;
* funding by year;
* funding by faculty;
* funding by study program;
* funding by business category;
* funding by source.

Financial information must be permission-protected.

# 12. MENTORING MANAGEMENT

**Problem → Intervention → Follow-Up → Result**

This must become part of the group's entrepreneurship journey.

* expiration date;
* uploaded document;

* verified by.

Status:

**Not Available → In Process → Submitted → Under Verification → Verified**

Support expiration reminders where applicable.

Dashboard should calculate legality coverage percentages.

# 15. REVENUE & BUSINESS DEVELOPMENT TRACKING

This module measures entrepreneurship outcomes.

Allow students to periodically submit:

* reporting period;
* revenue;
* number of transactions;
* products sold;
* number of customers;
* business expenses;
* estimated profit;
* number of workers;
* marketing coverage;
* notes.

Do not overwrite historical data.

Store periodic records to allow time-series analysis.

Generate:

* monthly revenue trends;
* quarterly trends;
* annual trends;
* before-program vs after-program comparison;
* growth rate;
* active business rate;
* business survival indicators.

All financial figures must have clearly defined reporting periods.

# 16. ENTREPRENEURSHIP JOURNEY

Every business must have a visual timeline.

Example:

2026

✓ Proposal Submitted
✓ Passed Selection
✓ Attended Training
✓ Received Funding
✓ Mentoring Completed
✓ Obtained NIB
✓ Product Verified
✓ Participated in Demo Day
✓ Participated in Festival
✓ Listed on MENOKEN Market
✓ Revenue Increased

Generate journey events automatically from verified system activities whenever possible.

Avoid requiring users to manually duplicate data already recorded elsewhere.

---

# 17. ENTREPRENEURSHIP GROWTH SCORE

Implement a configurable:

# MENOKEN GROWTH SCORE

Score range:

**0–100**

Potential dimensions:

* Legalities
* Product Development
* Sales
* Revenue Growth
* Digitalization
* Financial Management
* Innovation
* Program Participation
* Partnerships
* Business Sustainability

IMPORTANT:

Do NOT hard-code weights.

Create configurable score structures such as:

* score_indicators
* score_components
* score_weights
* score_rules
* score_results

Admin must be able to change weights and score thresholds.

Default classification can be:

0–20 → Idea
21–40 → Early Stage
41–60 → Growing
61–80 → Established
81–100 → Scale-Up

These ranges must remain configurable.

Every score should be explainable.

Show users which indicators contributed to their score.

---

# 22. DATA VERIFICATION & DATA QUALITY

Critical institutional data must use verification workflows.

Use the general lifecycle:

**Draft → Submitted → Under Review → Verified → Published**

Not every module must publish data publicly.

"Published" means approved for public or official display where applicable.

Examples requiring verification:

* legalities;
* products displayed publicly;
* revenue used for official reporting;
* funding reports;
* selected documentation;
* impact stories.

IMPORTANT:

Student-submitted data must NOT automatically become official university data.

Executive dashboards should primarily use verified data.

Show verification status clearly.

---

# 23. STORY OF IMPACT

Allow businesses to create structured impact stories.

Structure:

### BEFORE PROGRAM

Initial business condition.

### INTERVENTION

Training, funding, mentoring or other support received.

### AFTER PROGRAM

Changes following participation.

### IMPACT

Economic, social, environmental or employment impact.

Store supporting evidence and documentation where available.

Stories intended for public display require approval.

---

# 24. MENOKEN MARKET

Create a public-facing digital product catalog called:

# MENOKEN MARKET

MENOKEN Market is NOT initially a full e-commerce platform.

Use a:

**Social Commerce / Marketplace Aggregator**

Visitors can browse approved products.

Search/filter by:

* category;
* faculty;
* study program;
* product name;
* business name.

Product page:

* images;
* product name;
* price;
* business name;
* description;
* approved public contact;
* social media;
* purchase link.

External purchase channels may include:

* WhatsApp Business
* Instagram
* Shopee
* Tokopedia
* TikTok Shop
* Business website
* Other external platforms

Do NOT implement payment gateway in the initial phase.

Only verified/published products should appear publicly.

Admin dashboard should focus on actions.

Examples:

* proposals awaiting verification;

* overdue funding reports;
* mentoring follow-ups;
* upcoming training;
* upcoming Demo Day;
* upcoming Festival;
* documents approaching expiration.

Make dashboard cards clickable and lead to relevant filtered records.

Display metrics such as:

Provide filters:

* year;

Charts may include:

* year-over-year entrepreneurship growth;
* businesses by faculty;
* businesses by category;
* business-stage distribution;
* funding trend;
* revenue trend;
* legality development;
* active/inactive businesses;
* growth-score distribution.

Avoid decorative charts with no decision-making value.

# 29. NOTIFICATION & REMINDER ENGINE

Create an internal notification architecture.

Examples:

* Monthly revenue data has not been updated.
* NIB document has not been uploaded.
* A legality document is approaching expiration.
* Training starts tomorrow.
* Proposal status changed.
* Funding report is overdue.
* Festival starts in seven days.
* Mentoring follow-up is overdue.

Initial implementation:

**In-App Notifications**

Design architecture so future integrations can support:

* Email
* WhatsApp

Do not require external messaging integration in the initial build.

---

# 30. DATABASE ARCHITECTURE

Use a relational database architecture.

Preferred:

# PostgreSQL

Design normalized relational entities.

Minimum conceptual entities:

* users
* roles
* permissions
* faculties
* study_programs
* students
* business_groups
* business_members
* business_categories
* entrepreneurship_programs
* program_participations
* proposals
* reviewers
* review_assignments
* review_scores
* trainings
* training_participants
* attendance
* funding
* funding_usage
* mentoring_sessions
* mentoring_followups
* legality_types
* business_legalities
* products
* revenue_records
* business_development_records
* demo_days
* demo_day_participants
* festivals
* festival_participants
* partnerships
* documents
* impact_stories
* score_indicators
* score_weights
* score_results
* notifications
* verification_history
* audit_logs

Use:

* primary keys;
* foreign keys;
* unique constraints;
* appropriate indexes;
* created_at;
* updated_at;
* created_by where applicable;
* updated_by where applicable.

Use soft-delete or archival strategy for institutional records where appropriate.

Never delete historical entrepreneurship performance data casually.

---

# 31. DATA INTEGRITY

Avoid duplicated data.

Examples:

Faculty must reference faculty master data.

Study program must reference study-program master data.

Students should reference one student record rather than repeatedly storing student identity in every module.

Products must reference business groups.

Revenue must reference business groups and reporting periods.

Legalities must reference business groups and legality types.

Program participation must connect business groups with entrepreneurship programs.

Maintain referential integrity.

---

# 32. AUDIT TRAIL

Institutional data changes must be traceable.

Track important operations:

* created;
* submitted;
* reviewed;
* verified;
* rejected;
* updated;
* published;
* archived.

Where appropriate record:

* user;
* action;
* entity;
* entity ID;
* timestamp;
* previous state;
* new state.

Audit history must be protected from ordinary users.

---

# 33. SECURITY

Implement secure architecture.

Requirements:

* secure authentication;
* password hashing;
* RBAC;
* authorization on backend/server side;
* protected routes;
* secure file access;
* input validation;
* output sanitization where needed;
* secure session/token handling;
* rate limiting where appropriate;
* safe upload validation;
* protection against unauthorized record access.

Never rely solely on hiding UI buttons for security.

Backend/API authorization must enforce permissions.

Never expose:

* passwords;
* authentication secrets;
* private documents;
* unnecessary student personal information;
* private financial records

to public users.

---

# 34. PRIVACY

Apply data minimization.

Public MENOKEN Market should display only information explicitly intended for publication.

Leadership dashboards should prioritize aggregated information.

Detailed student data should be accessible only to authorized roles.

Separate:

**Internal Data**

from

**Public Data**

at the data/business-logic level.

---

# 35. UI/UX DESIGN SYSTEM

MENOKEN must feel like an entrepreneurship and innovation platform rather than a traditional government administration application.

Design principles:

# CLEAN

# MODERN

# YOUTHFUL

# PROFESSIONAL

# ENTREPRENEURIAL

# DATA-DRIVEN

# PAPUA / UNCEN IDENTITY

The visual identity may incorporate subtle inspiration from Papua and Universitas Cenderawasih, but avoid excessive decorative ethnic motifs that reduce readability.

Use:

* clean cards;
* strong typography hierarchy;
* modern tables;
* useful icons;
* product photography;
* meaningful charts;
* progress indicators;
* timelines;
* status badges;
* empty states;
* loading states;
* validation feedback;
* responsive navigation.

Avoid:

* excessive gradients;
* excessive animation;
* cluttered dashboards;
* oversized cards;
* random colors;
* unnecessary glassmorphism;
* decorative charts;
* admin-template appearance.

Use one coherent design system across the entire application.

---

# 36. RESPONSIVE DESIGN

MENOKEN must work properly on:

* Desktop
* Laptop
* Tablet
* Smartphone

Student-facing workflows must be mobile-first.

Students should comfortably update:

* profiles;
* products;
* revenue;
* legalities;
* documents;
* mentoring follow-ups

from smartphones.

Tables must remain usable on small screens through responsive patterns.

---

# 37. USER EXPERIENCE

Do not force users to repeatedly enter the same data.

Auto-populate information from existing master/profile data whenever possible.

Examples:

Selecting a student should automatically retrieve faculty/study program information where available.

Selecting a business group should retrieve its identity.

Program participation should connect to the existing group profile.

Journey events should be generated from existing verified activities.

Use progressive forms for long workflows.

Implement autosave/draft behavior where appropriate.

Provide clear status feedback after submission.

---

# 38. EMPTY STATES & REAL DATA

During development, seed realistic demonstration data when required.

However:

DO NOT create misleading fake analytics.

DO NOT hard-code KPI numbers into dashboard components.

All dashboard metrics must ultimately be calculated from database records.

Seed/demo data must be clearly separable from production data.

When database records are empty, show meaningful empty states rather than fake statistics.

---

# 39. SYSTEM PERFORMANCE

Design for future growth.

Use:

* pagination;
* filtering;
* indexed database queries;
* lazy loading where appropriate;
* optimized images;
* efficient aggregate queries;
* reusable components.

Do not fetch the entire database for dashboard calculations.

---

# 40. DEVELOPMENT ARCHITECTURE

Use a modular and maintainable architecture.

Separate:

* UI components;
* pages/routes;
* domain modules;
* business logic;
* API/services;
* database access;
* validation;
* authentication;
* authorization;
* reporting;
* analytics.

Avoid creating one huge frontend file or one huge backend file.

Use clear naming conventions.

Avoid unnecessary dependencies.

---

# 41. ERROR HANDLING

Implement proper:

* form validation;
* server-side validation;
* API error handling;
* upload errors;
* authorization errors;
* empty states;
* retry states;
* user-friendly error messages.

Never silently fail.

Do not display raw technical stack traces to ordinary users.

---

# 42. DEVELOPMENT PHASES

MENOKEN must be developed incrementally.

## PHASE 0 — FOUNDATION

Build:

* project architecture;
* database schema;
* authentication;
* RBAC;
* master data;
* file-storage architecture;
* audit architecture;
* verification architecture;
* design system;
* navigation;
* base responsive layout.

## PHASE 1 — CORE ENTREPRENEURSHIP SYSTEM

Build:

* students;
* business groups;
* members;
* business profiles;
* entrepreneurship programs;
* program participation;
* proposals;
* reviewers;
* selection;
* basic operational dashboard.

The main goal:

# ONE GROUP – ONE DIGITAL PROFILE must work end-to-end.

## PHASE 2 — DEVELOPMENT & MONITORING

Add:

* training;
* attendance;
* funding;
* funding utilization;
* mentoring;
* legalities;
* products;
* revenue;
* documentation;
* notifications.

## PHASE 3 — PERFORMANCE & ANALYTICS

Add:

* MENOKEN Growth Score;
* entrepreneurship journey;
* executive dashboard;
* KPI;
* year-over-year analytics;
* impact measurement;
* Story of Impact;
* automated reporting.

## PHASE 4 — MENOKEN MARKET

Add:

* public product catalog;
* product search;
* product filtering;
* business public profile;
* WhatsApp integration/link;
* social media links;
* external marketplace links.

## PHASE 5 — ENTREPRENEURSHIP ECOSYSTEM

Add:

* mentor network;
* alumni entrepreneur network;
* business partners;
* government partners;
* investors;
* collaboration opportunities;
* advanced networking;
* ecosystem analytics.

---

# 43. CRITICAL DEVELOPMENT RULE

DO NOT attempt to implement all five phases simultaneously.

Start with:

# PHASE 0 + PHASE 1 ONLY.

Before writing large amounts of UI code:

1. analyze requirements;
2. propose the system architecture;
3. design the relational database;
4. map roles and permissions;
5. map core workflows;
6. define module boundaries;
7. identify dependencies;
8. create implementation plan.

Then implement Phase 0 and Phase 1.

Do NOT create Phase 2–5 as fake modules simply to make the application appear complete.

Future modules may appear in planning documentation, but they must not be represented as functional unless actually implemented.

---

# 44. NO PLACEHOLDER DEVELOPMENT

STRICT RULE:

Do NOT replace required functionality with:

* static pages;
* fake buttons;
* hard-coded dashboard numbers;
* fake CRUD;
* non-persistent forms;
* placeholder charts;
* fake uploads;
* simulated authentication;
* front-end-only authorization;
* hard-coded scoring.

Every feature declared as functional must actually work.

Buttons must perform their intended action.

Forms must persist valid data.

Filters must affect data.

Dashboards must calculate database records.

Role restrictions must be enforced.

---

# 45. ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA FOR PHASE 0 + PHASE 1

Phase 0 and Phase 1 are considered successful only when:

1. Users can authenticate securely.
2. Roles and permissions work.
3. Super Admin can manage master data.
4. Admin can manage students.
5. Admin/student can create a business group according to permissions.
6. Members can be linked to the group.
7. One Group–One Digital Profile works.
8. Admin can create an entrepreneurship program.
9. A group can participate in a program.
10. Student/group can create and submit a proposal.
11. Admin can verify proposal administration.
12. Reviewer can access only assigned proposals.
13. Reviewer can submit scores.
14. Weighted scoring is calculated correctly.
15. Admin can determine selection status.
16. Status history is retained.
17. Core dashboard reads actual database records.
18. Important actions generate audit history.
19. Unauthorized users cannot access restricted data.
20. UI works on desktop and mobile.
21. Refreshing the browser does not lose persisted data.
22. No critical workflow depends on hard-coded mock data.

**MENOKEN Growth Score** — entrepreneurship development score.

**MENOKEN Journey** — business entrepreneurship timeline.

**MENOKEN Insight** — executive analytics/dashboard if a dedicated name is needed.

Do NOT introduce unrelated competing product names without approval.

---

# 47. LOCAL CONTEXT

MENOKEN is intended for Universitas Cenderawasih and should support entrepreneurship in Papua.

Business categories and data structures must be flexible enough to accommodate:

* culinary businesses;
* creative economy;
* digital businesses;
* agriculture;
* fisheries;
* livestock;
* forestry-related products where appropriate;
* tourism;
* education;
* crafts;
* local Papua products;
* services;
* technology;
* household industries;
* other emerging categories.

Do not stereotype Papua visually or functionally.

Local identity should enhance the product without limiting its professional institutional character.

---

# 48. EXPECTED SYSTEM OUTCOME

MENOKEN should eventually allow university leadership to answer questions such as:

* How many students are entrepreneurs?
* How many business groups exist?
* Which faculties and study programs participate?
* What types of businesses are being developed?
* How many businesses remain active?
* How many businesses are growing?
* How many products have been created?
* How many businesses have legalities?
* How much funding has been distributed?
* What are the funding sources?
* How many students participated in entrepreneurship development activities?
* How many businesses received mentoring?
* How much revenue has been generated?
* Is revenue growing?
* How many businesses survive after programs end?
* How many jobs have been created?
* How many partnerships have been established?
* Which businesses show the strongest development?
* What measurable impact has the university entrepreneurship program produced?
* How has entrepreneurship performance changed year-over-year?

---

# 49. FOUR PRIMARY OUTPUTS

MENOKEN must ultimately produce:

## 1. ENTREPRENEURSHIP DATABASE

An integrated database of student entrepreneurship.

## 2. ENTREPRENEURSHIP TRACKING SYSTEM

A longitudinal tracking system for each business group.

## 3. MENOKEN MARKET

A public digital catalog promoting verified student products and businesses.

## 4. ENTREPRENEURSHIP PERFORMANCE DASHBOARD

A decision-support dashboard measuring:

* activity;
* output;
* outcome;
* growth;
* sustainability;
* impact.

---

# 50. FINAL PRODUCT PRINCIPLE

The most important principle of MENOKEN is:

> **Every activity generates data.
> Every relevant data point generates an indicator.
> Every indicator provides insight.
> Every insight supports better decisions.**

MENOKEN should become the institutional source of truth for student entrepreneurship at Universitas Cenderawasih.

The platform should help Universitas Cenderawasih move from simply reporting entrepreneurship activities toward demonstrating measurable entrepreneurship development and impact.

1|from fastapi import FastAPI, APIRouter
2|from dotenv import load_dotenv
3|from starlette.middleware.cors import CORSMiddleware
4|from motor.motor_asyncio import AsyncIOMotorClient
5|import os
6|import logging
7|from pathlib import Path
8|from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, ConfigDict
9|from typing import List
10|import uuid
11|from datetime import datetime, timezone
12|
13|
14|ROOT_DIR = Path(__file__).parent
15|load_dotenv(ROOT_DIR / '.env')
16|
17|# MongoDB connection
18|mongo_url = os.environ['MONGO_URL']
19|client = AsyncIOMotorClient(mongo_url)
20|db = client[os.environ['DB_NAME']]
21|
22|# Create the main app without a prefix
23|app = FastAPI()
24|
25|# Create a router with the /api prefix
26|api_router = APIRouter(prefix="/api")
27|
28|
29|# Define Models
30|class StatusCheck(BaseModel):
31|    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="ignore")  # Ignore MongoDB's _id field
32|    
33|    id: str = Field(default_factory=lambda: str(uuid.uuid4()))
34|    client_name: str
35|    timestamp: datetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc))
36|
37|class StatusCheckCreate(BaseModel):
38|    client_name: str
39|
40|# Add your routes to the router instead of directly to app
41|@api_router.get("/")
42|async def root():
43|    return {"message": "Hello World"}
44|
45|@api_router.post("/status", response_model=StatusCheck)
46|async def create_status_check(input: StatusCheckCreate):
47|    status_dict = input.model_dump()
48|    status_obj = StatusCheck(**status_dict)
49|    
50|    # Convert to dict and serialize datetime to ISO string for MongoDB
51|    doc = status_obj.model_dump()
52|    doc['timestamp'] = doc['timestamp'].isoformat()
53|    
54|    _ = await db.status_checks.insert_one(doc)
55|    return status_obj
56|
57|@api_router.get("/status", response_model=List[StatusCheck])
58|async def get_status_checks():
59|    # Exclude MongoDB's _id field from the query results
60|    status_checks = await db.status_checks.find({}, {"_id": 0}).to_list(1000)
61|    
62|    # Convert ISO string timestamps back to datetime objects
63|    for check in status_checks:
64|        if isinstance(check['timestamp'], str):
65|            check['timestamp'] = datetime.fromisoformat(check['timestamp'])
66|    
67|    return status_checks
68|
69|# Include the router in the main app
70|app.include_router(api_router)
71|
72|app.add_middleware(
73|    CORSMiddleware,
74|    allow_credentials=True,
75|    allow_origins=os.environ.get('CORS_ORIGINS', '*').split(','),
76|    allow_methods=["*"],
77|    allow_headers=["*"],
78|)
79|
80|# Configure logging
81|logging.basicConfig(
82|    level=logging.INFO,
83|    format='%(asctime)s - %(name)s - %(levelname)s - %(message)s'
84|)
85|logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
86|
87|@app.on_event("shutdown")
88|async def shutdown_db_client():
89|    client.close()

===FILE: /app/frontend/src/App.js
/app/frontend/src/App.js:
1|import { useEffect } from "react";
2|import "@/App.css";
3|import { BrowserRouter, Routes, Route } from "react-router-dom";
4|import axios from "axios";
5|import { HOME } from "@/constants/testIds";
6|
7|const BACKEND_URL = process.env.REACT_APP_BACKEND_URL;
8|const API = `${BACKEND_URL}/api`;
9|
10|const Home = () => {
11|  const helloWorldApi = async () => {
12|    try {
13|      const response = await axios.get(`${API}/`);
14|      console.log(response.data.message);
15|    } catch (e) {
16|      console.error(e, `errored out requesting / api`);
17|    }
18|  };
19|
20|  useEffect(() => {
21|    helloWorldApi();
22|  }, []);
23|
24|  return (
25|    // The marker attribute below lets the platform probe detect the stock splash — remove it with this page
26|    <div data-emergent-splash>
27|      <header className="App-header">
28|        <a
29|          data-testid={HOME.emergentLink}
30|          className="App-link"
31|          href="https://emergent.sh"
32|          target="_blank"
33|          rel="noopener noreferrer"
34|        >
35|          <img src="https://avatars.githubusercontent.com/in/1201222?s=120&u=2686cf91179bbafbc7a71bfbc43004cf9ae1acea&v=4" />
36|        </a>
37|        <p className="mt-5">Building something incredible ~!</p>
38|      </header>
39|    </div>
40|  );
41|};
42|
43|function App() {
44|  return (
45|    <div className="App">
46|      <BrowserRouter>
47|        <Routes>
48|          <Route path="/" element={<Home />}>
49|            <Route index element={<Home />} />
50|          </Route>
51|        </Routes>
52|      </BrowserRouter>
53|    </div>
54|  );
55|}
56|
57|export default App;
58|

1|import React from "react";
2|import ReactDOM from "react-dom/client";
3|import { QueryClient, QueryClientProvider } from "@tanstack/react-query";
4|import "@/index.css";
5|import App from "@/App";
6|
7|const queryClient = new QueryClient({
8|  defaultOptions: {
9|    queries: {
10|      staleTime: 60_000,
11|      refetchOnWindowFocus: false,
12|    },
13|  },
14|});
15|
16|const root = ReactDOM.createRoot(document.getElementById("root"));
17|root.render(
18|  <React.StrictMode>
19|    <QueryClientProvider client={queryClient}>
20|      <App />
21|    </QueryClientProvider>
22|  </React.StrictMode>,
23|);

14|function makeDevServerV5Compatible(devServerConfig) {
15|  const {
16|    https,
17|    onAfterSetupMiddleware,
18|    onBeforeSetupMiddleware,
19|    onListening,
20|    setupMiddlewares,
21|    ...compatibleConfig
22|  } = devServerConfig;
23|
24|  compatibleConfig.server =
25|    typeof https === "object"
26|      ? { type: "https", options: https }
27|      : https
28|        ? "https"
29|        : "http";
30|  compatibleConfig.headers = {
31|    ...compatibleConfig.headers,
32|    "Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy": "same-origin",
33|  };
34|
35|  if (onBeforeSetupMiddleware || setupMiddlewares) {
36|    compatibleConfig.setupMiddlewares = (middlewares, devServer) => {
37|      if (onBeforeSetupMiddleware) {
38|        onBeforeSetupMiddleware(devServer);
39|      }
40|
41|      return setupMiddlewares
42|        ? setupMiddlewares(middlewares, devServer)
43|        : middlewares;
44|    };
45|  }
46|
47|  compatibleConfig.onListening = (devServer) => {
48|    devServer.close ??= (callback) => devServer.stopCallback(callback);
49|
50|    if (onListening) {
51|      onListening(devServer);
52|    }
53|    if (onAfterSetupMiddleware) {
54|      onAfterSetupMiddleware(devServer);
55|    }
56|  };
57|
58|  return compatibleConfig;
59|}

165|    webpackConfig = withVisualEdits(webpackConfig);

183|  // the dev server. Warns once, then this path stops calling it.
184|  let overlay = emergentOverlay;
185|  const overlayFailed = (site, err) => {
186|    overlay = undefined;
187|    console.warn(
188|      `[emergent-overlay] ${site} failed — overlay disabled:`,
189|      err instanceof Error ? err.message : err,
190|    );
191|  };

193|  webpackConfig.devServer = (devServerConfig) => {
194|    devServerConfig = devServerBeforeOverlay(devServerConfig);
195|
196|    // Overlay owns runtime errors; webpack keeps compile errors.
197|    try {
198|      devServerConfig = overlay.devServer(devServerConfig);
199|    } catch (err) {
200|      overlayFailed("devServer config", err);
201|    }
202|
203|    const previousSetupMiddlewares = devServerConfig.setupMiddlewares;
204|
205|    devServerConfig.setupMiddlewares = (middlewares, devServer) => {
206|      // Registered ahead of the chain's own body parsers, which would consume the raw stream
207|      // the overlay reads. Adapter taking a pre-parsed req.body is the overlay-side fix.
208|      try {
209|        if (overlay) overlay.attach(devServer);
210|      } catch (err) {
211|        overlayFailed("attach", err);
212|      }
213|
214|      if (previousSetupMiddlewares) {
215|        middlewares = previousSetupMiddlewares(middlewares, devServer);
216|      }
217|
218|      return middlewares;
219|    };
220|
221|    return devServerConfig;
222|  };
223|}

| Rice | 15, 30 & 45 DAP | 1 tbsp / 15 L (spray) |
| Corn | 14 & 35 DAP | 1 tbsp / 15 L (spray) |
| Leafy Vegetables | Every 10 days | 1 tsp / 5 L (pour) |
| Chili & Tomato | Every 2 weeks | 1 tbsp / 15 L (spray) |
| Palm Oil | Every 3 months | 50 g / tree (sprinkle) |
| Coffee & Cocoa | Every 2 months | 30 g / tree (sprinkle) |
| Fruit Trees | Monthly | 30–50 g / tree |
| Ornamentals | Every 2 weeks | 1 tsp / 5 L (pour) |

tbsp = tablespoon, tsp = teaspoon, DAP = days after planting. Free consultation via WhatsApp for large fields.

Alur Transaksi

## Dua jalur, satu wadah.

Retail langsung untuk konsumen. Kontrak & escrow untuk industri. Semua dana dilindungi hingga barang diterima.

B2C · Retail

### Katalog → Keranjang → Escrow → Kirim

1. 1\. Pembeli pilih produk PIRT/olahan desa
2. 2\. Checkout dengan kurir reguler/instan
3. 3\. Bayar via Stripe / gateway aman
4. 4\. BUMDes kemas & kirim, escrow menahan dana
5. 5\. Barang diterima → dana cair ke BUMDes

B2B · Grosir

### RFQ → Negosiasi → Kontrak → Escrow → BAST

1. 1\. Buyer isi RFQ (tonase, jadwal, spek)
2. 2\. Chat negosiasi harga & standar mutu
3. 3\. Draf kontrak digital & invoice B2B
4. 4\. DP/pelunasan ditampung di escrow
5. 5\. Kargo → QC → BAST → dana cair

[Ajukan RFQ Sekarang](https://agro-commerce-35.emergent.host/rfq)

Saya sudah meninjau kedua situs. Ringkasan singkat:

- **agro-commerce-35.emergent.host** — marketplace produk desa nasional (BUMDes/UMKM): hero + statistik real-time, grid kategori berbasis gambar, kartu produk (lokasi, harga, **badge legalitas NIB/PIRT/SNI**), halaman badan usaha, dan alur transaksi **B2C (katalog→keranjang→escrow)** + **B2B (RFQ→kontrak→escrow→BAST)**. Ini sangat selaras dengan **MENOKEN Market** + modul Produk.
- **agrodyke.com** — landing page pemasaran satu produk: hero kuat, galeri video, langkah "cara pakai", kartu ukuran/harga, tabel dosis, showcase sertifikasi, testimoni before/after, FAQ, order via **WhatsApp**. Ini gaya visual/landing & social-commerce (tanpa payment gateway).

Keduanya bisa diadopsi untuk MENOKEN, tapi sebagian besar berada di **Fase 4 (MENOKEN Market)** dan **modul Produk (Fase 14)** — yang tadinya belum kita bangun. Sebelum lanjut, mohon konfirmasi:

1. Apa yang ingin diadopsi?
   a. Desain & fitur **marketplace/katalog produk** (dari agro-commerce) untuk MENOKEN Market
   b. Gaya visual **landing/pemasaran + galeri + testimoni + order WhatsApp** (dari agrodyke)
   c. Keduanya

2. Untuk marketplace, sejauh mana fiturnya sekarang?
   a. Katalog publik + kartu produk + badge legalitas + filter kategori/fakultas + order via WhatsApp/link eksternal (social-commerce, **tanpa payment gateway** — sesuai master prompt)
   b. Termasuk alur B2C escrow + B2B RFQ/kontrak (butuh payment gateway seperti Stripe — di luar spec awal)

3. Ini berarti saya mulai membangun modul **Produk + MENOKEN Market** sekarang (menambah Fase 4), sementara Fase 0+1 tetap utuh. Setuju?
   a. Ya, lanjutkan bangun MENOKEN Market sekarang
   b. Hanya perbarui tampilan/landing dulu, marketplace nanti

4. Untuk order produk, saluran yang dipakai:
   a. WhatsApp Business + Instagram + Shopee/Tokopedia/TikTok (link eksternal saja)
   b. Perlu checkout & pembayaran internal (Stripe)
